64%、台灣 36%。

AM 產業長期正向

永豐觀點 
預估 2027 年 AM 營收增長 5~8%、OEM 年增 1~3%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進建議 ：預估 2026、2027 年營收為 242.9、261 億元(-3.2%、+7.5%YoY)、
毛利率 32.7%、34%，營業利益為 40.5、49 億元(-12%、+21%YoY)，稅後淨利
為 36.1、40.2 億元(-4.9%、+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 6.12、6.79 元。看好東陽最差
情況已過，即將迎來旺季，出貨將逐步重返正常增長軌道。目前評價交易在
13X2026 年 EPS(F)、11.9X2027 年 EPS(F)，為近五年本益比區間(10~18X)下緣，
本次以 2027 年 EPS 進行評價，給予 15X 本益比，目標價 102 元(15X2027 年(F) 
EPS 6.79 元)，維持買進建議。

ESG 評析 
東陽企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

### Page 2

公司簡介：東陽成立於 1967 年，主營項目為汽、機車零組件，為台灣塑膠件供應
商，也是全球第一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球市佔率 70%、板金部份
市佔率 38%。2Q26 AM 營收佔比 75.68%，OEM 為 24.32%。AM 營收分布為北
美 71%、中南美 11%、歐洲 11%、中東 3%。OEM 市場為中國 64%、台灣 36%。
1995 年起，公司陸續在中國重慶、南京、福州、長春、哈爾濱、敦陽、長春、天
津、武漢、長沙、廣州等地設廠，中國 OEM(非合併)主要客戶包含一汽 64%、特
斯拉 11%、長安汽車 10%、廣汽 4%。 
關稅底定，北美 AM 客戶回補庫存，3Q26 AM 營收年增 15.7%，重回成長：美
國 5 月下旬將台灣輸美汽車零件的 232 條款關稅調降至 15% 後，客戶結束觀
望，回補先前壓低的庫存，拉貨逐月增加，目前東陽工廠稼動率逾 90%，採兩班
制 24 小時生產。3Q26 AM 營收 45 億元(+5% QoQ、+15.7% YoY)，9 月 AM 營
收 14.96 億元(+0.85% MoM、+7.6% YoY)。 
AM 產業長期正面：北美維修量降幅收斂、副廠件占比創高；北美旺季即將到
來，客戶補庫存可期：北美碰撞維修量已從 2025 年的大幅衰退回到接近持平，
副廠件占比創新高，產業面已落底，結構變化對東陽長期有利： 
1. 汽車維修產業面：維修量衰退已收斂，且AM 件占比創新高：依美國理賠估價
平台 CCC Intelligent Solutions 統計(下簡稱 CCC)，2026 年 1~9 月全美理
賠件數降幅由去年同期的 8.3%縮小至 3.5%，可修復估價件數由 10.4%縮小
至 5.4%。北美大型汽車維修連鎖企業 Boyd 估計 2Q26 可修復理賠量持平至
年減 2%，與 CCC 方向一致。另，依 CCC 統計，2025 年副廠件占全美碰撞
換件金額 22.7%，為歷史新高；在維修量下滑的環境下，副廠件金額仍成長  
1.7%，原廠件則減少 1.8%。綜合上述，產業面已落底，有望回溫，而結構方
面，副廠件使用率持續提升，東陽為受惠者。 
2. 東陽客戶面：主要客戶 LKQ 於北美 AM 碰撞件優於市場：LKQ 2Q26 北美
零件及服務有機營收成長 0.5%，為九季來首度轉正，其中 AM 碰撞件營收
成長約 2%；同時，LKQ 公布其替代零件使用率(APU，即維修時選用副廠件、
而非原廠件的比例)連續兩季創下歷史新高，已突破 40%，背後原因是美國保
險公司近期理賠成本上升壓力較大，加上二手車價格走高，促使保險公司更傾

營運現況與分析 
圖一：東陽主要生產汽車塑膠及鈑金件

資料來源：東陽，Oct. 2026

### Page 3

3

向要求維修廠使用價格較低的副廠零件以控制成本 ， 顯示副廠零件滲透率提升
是長期趨勢，而非短期景氣波動。 
3. 研究部研判：AM 最差情況已過。維修量降幅收斂、副廠件占比提升，加上關
稅底定後的補庫存，再加上東陽每年投入平均約 18 億元開發新模具(今年為
25 億元)、持續擴大北美與歐洲保險公司認證，支撐 AM 營收回到成長軌道、
長期向好。惟 4Q25 受暴風雪帶動、基期較高，若今年冬天偏暖，則 4Q26
年增幅度可能有限；1Q27 則因 1Q26 低基期，年增表現較有利。 
短期負面因子：貨櫃仍偏緊，緩解速度取決於旺季強度：目前待出貨訂單累積 200
多櫃訂單，等待客戶安排貨櫃艙位，緩解速度需視旺季強度(採 FOB 報價，過往客
戶在需求好的時候願意加價取得貨櫃)。研究處認為目前客戶庫存約 1 個月，且旺
季(11 月~隔年 3 月)到來，預期有望逐步緩解。 
4Q26 毛利率有機會優於 3Q26：旺季稼動率有機會達 90%~95%(目前東陽工廠
稼動率逾 90%)，可攤提模具折舊等固定成本(AM 模具折舊約占 AM 成本 17%)。
旺季時北美銷售比重由淡季約 65%~66%回升至約 70%，有助產品組合。而市場
擔憂原物料上升的部分，截至九月來看，PP 較戰前上漲 5%，ABS 為 24%，但因
塑膠原料約 90%使用 PP、ABS 僅約 10%， 且東陽議價能力強 ， 成本上漲可轉嫁，
今年以來東陽產品售價已調整約 1.1%~1.5%，占比近 30%的自有產品轉嫁較快，
其餘產品約需一季~半年轉嫁，綜合以上，原物料上漲的部分對整體毛利率影響不
大。主要影響毛利率的因素在於產品組合(北美營收佔比、塑膠件比重)，以及稼動
率，故預期 4Q26、1Q27AM 毛利率將有望回升至 40%以上。 
預估 2027 年東陽 AM 營收重回正常成長軌道，年增 5~8%；OEM 年增 1~3%：
東陽是全球第一大汽車 AM 塑膠件製造商，塑膠件全球市佔率 70%、板金部份市
佔率 38%，其品項覆蓋範圍最廣，且模具持續增加，故在市況不明朗時，東陽仍
佔據產業領先地位及強議價能力，東陽2026 年預估將有 500~600 項 AM 新品通
過認證，新產品毛利率較高，搭配 Q4~Q1 迎來傳統旺季，預估 AM 營收有望持
續回溫。AM 市場的競爭重點便是模具數及規模經濟，參考東陽於 2024-2025 年
擴大模具開發套數及擴充產能，將有助於未來幾年 AM 營收的增長。而 OEM 方
面，台灣車市有望逐步復甦，且東陽客戶新案陸續放量，有望支撐2027 年營運表
現。中國 OEM 方面，今明年重點在於電動車轉型持續發酵，客戶展望正面，雖然
前幾年陸續關廠導致營收規模持續縮減， 但近期已調整告一段落， 預期整體產能利
用率能夠提升，推升毛利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估2027 年東陽 AM 營收重
回正常成長軌道，年增 5~8%；OEM 年增 1~3%。 
維持買進建議，長線投資價值顯現：預估 2026、2027 年營收為 242.9、261 億元
(-3.2%、+7.5%YoY)、毛利率 32.7%、34%，營業利益為 40.5、49 億元(-12%、
+21%YoY)，稅後淨利為 36.1、40.2 億元(-4.9%、+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 6.12、
表一：北美維修量止跌、副廠件占比創高

可修復理賠量 2Q26 持平至年減 2%

國巨* (2327 TT) 
淡季不淡，第四季續強

永豐觀點 
AI 相關占比達 20%，較上季提升 4%。稼動率方面，將較上季 95%持續成長，拋
開過往明顯淡旺季，營運成長可期。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進投資建議：主係看好國巨營運將受惠於(1) 日韓廠商將低階 MLCC 產能
移轉至高階產品的訂單外溢效應，(2) 預估 4Q26 稼動率逾 95%。另一方面，漲價
效應浮現將進一步推升毛利率表現， (3) AI 伺服器電源規格提升下，國巨在 AI 
Server 產品線齊全，除 MLCC 外，鉭質電容、電感及晶片電阻的用量增加。綜上
所述，國巨受惠轉單效應、被動元件產業循環向上、仍有持續漲價機會，故維持買
進的投資建議，以 2027-2028 年平均 EPS 預估，給予 27 倍本益比，目標價 940
元。

ESG 評析 
國巨企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

國巨集團為台灣被動元件第一大供應商：3Q26 營收產品別占比 MLCC 21.5%，
Magnetics 22.3%，T antalum(Kemet) 23.7%，Resistor 15.3%，Sensor 10.6%，
Other Components 6.6%，其中 MLCC 與電阻成長主要來自標準品與特殊品需求
強勁 ； 終端應用占比 ：Industrial 28.7%，Computing & Enterprise System 28.3%，
Automotive 14.9%，Consumer 11.7%，T elecom 12.5%，Aerospace/Defense 
/Medical 3.9%，其中 AI 佔比約為 20%，較上季增加 4%。 
價格調漲及稼動率提升，3Q26 獲利優於預期：受惠於 AI 相關及工業 Industrial
需求強勁，帶動各產品線、應用市場營收成長，其中標準品及特殊品皆成長，AI 相
關營收比重達 19.8%。國巨 3Q26 營收 459.32 億元(+11.4% QoQ，+49.7% YoY)，
受惠產品組合及價格調整，毛利率 41%(+2.5pts QoQ，+4.8pts YoY)，優於預期
的 39.5%，營利率 29.5%(+3.1ptsQoQ，+6.6ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 119.8 億元
(+27.2%QoQ，+88.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 5.83 元，優於預期的 5.27 元。 
動能延續，4Q26 規模經濟效應顯現：展望第四季，國巨的 B/B Ratio 約為 2，仍
處於歷史高檔水準。稼動率方面，預將優於上季的 95%。在稼動率持續提升且先
前與客戶的漲價協議陸續發酵下 ， 研究部預估國巨4Q26 營收 532.44 億元(+7.5% 
QoQ，+48% YoY)，受惠產品組合及價格調整，毛利率 41.5%(+0.5pts QoQ ，
+4.2pts YoY)，營利 29.8%(+0.3ptsQoQ，+5.5ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 132.39 億元
(+10.5%QoQ，+96.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 6.45 元 。 預估國巨2026 年營收 1853.98
億（+39.5%YoY） ，毛利率40%(+3.8pts YoY)，營利率28%(+5.6pts YoY)，稅後淨
利 426.4 億元(+80.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 20.76 元。 
產業循環向上空間可期：在 AI 伺服器電源規格不斷提升下，被動元件用量明顯增
加，日韓廠商皆發出漲價通知，國巨受惠於高階 MLCC 產品用量的增加外，鋁質
及鉭質電容等用量增加顯著。此外，預期在轉單效應顯現後，將使國巨在中低階產
品具有市場主導權。若以應用別區分，目前以 AI 相關終端需求最為強勁，國巨產
品亦將受惠鉭質電容 、 電感及晶片電阻的用量增加 ； 車用亦有復甦 。 展望2027 年，
國巨將於 2Q27 陸續開出新產能，預估總產能將年增 10-15%。此外，除 AI 相關
需求持續暢旺外，因競爭對手產能調整，產生的產能缺口難在短期補足，轉單效應
將至少延續至 2Q27，被動元件產業循環仍在持續向上，預期2027 年 MLCC 仍有
30%以上的漲價空間，其餘被動元件也有望因產能排擠使供給出現缺口，帶動下波
漲價 ， 並透過與客戶簽訂長期合約確保營運延續性 ， 故預估國巨2027 年營收 2422.2
億（+30.6%YoY），毛利率43.6%(+3.6pts YoY)，營利率 32%(+4pts YoY)，稅後

## Document 146: 261031_ubs_liteon

Date: 2026-10-31

### Page 1

ab 30 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Lite-On Technology
Cloud growth and margins stay healthy amid
supply chain constraints
Cloud growth reaffirmed; stronger margins cushion platform delays
Lite-On hosted its investor conference after the close Friday 10/30 and reaffirmed its full
year +70% cloud growth guidance. Sales were already reported slightly light at NT
$57.4bn, +8.9% slightly below UBS/Street +10.5%/14.3% estimates due to hardware

kitting issues and new platform delays. We continue to see strong growth with an
expanding customer base and strong ramp of compute but trim 2026/27E sales growth
from +31%/38% to +30%/35% to factor in component constraints and 800V
DC sidecar ramp more into 2028E due to engineering readiness. With the company
increasing its AI mix to 30% in 2026 and >40% in 2027 and ultimately ramping
HVDC in 2028, we expect it to sustain its current margin level, and maintain our Buy

rating with an unchanged NT$400 PT based on unchanged NT$20 2028E EPS.
Q3 growth constrained; margins expand on improving mix
Lite-On Q3 sales of  NT$57.4bn were slightly light with  Cloud/AIoT +57% YoY to 59% of
group revenue with growth capped by the components shortage, Opto-electro +17%
YoY (14% of sales) and  IT/CE -6% YoY (27% of sales) softer due to the weakening
consumer market more than offsetting the  strong SpaceX LEO power supply growth.

The group GM/OpM was better at 25.9%/15.1% vs. UBSe/Street 15.2%/13.8% on
better mix and operating leverage to lift Q3 EPS to NT$3.27 vs. UBSe/Street NT
$2.85/2.97. The company exited Q3 with NT$66bn inventory, +20% QoQ/119% YoY
due to the components mismatch  and buffer inventory build-ups with LTAs.
Multiple growth drivers to sustain the growth in 2027 before HVDC ramps
Although the industry supply constraints might push the 18.3kW power shelves toward

end of Q4, we are seeing the 8.5kW PSU and BBU for AMD servers and ASICs
supporting the cloud growth in 2026. With more capacity to come online in H127, we
are seeing the 50V power rack to start shipping with BBU to ramp more than 30% YoY
in 2027. The company is increasing its 2026 capex from NT$19.7bn vs. prior NT$18bn to
meet the substantial cloud demand and expecting even larger spending in 2027, to
double their cloud capacity by the end of 2027.

Reiterate Buy rating with unchanged PT of NT$400
Lite-On's shares have corrected 24% from their August peak due to the supply chain
concern on platforms delay and component constraints. We trim our 2026-27 sales
growth estimates from +31.3%/37.5% to +29.6%/34.8% while expecting it to
reaccelerate with the 800VDC power rack ramp in 2028E to support +32% growth. We
expect margins to stay on the growth trend and fine-tune our 2026-27 EPS
estimates from  NT$10.85/NT$16.00 to NT$11.23/NT$15.00 and 2028E EPS unchanged

at NT$20.00 with NT$400 PT based on an unchanged 20x 2028E EPS.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Lite-On Technology using PE methodology. Key risks to our investment thesis:
The principal risk is a slower AI infrastructure deployment cycle. Our industry work shows
deployed IT GW is the single-largest determinant of the power TAM, so a meaningful

reduction in hyperscaler accelerator deployment would affect all power suppliers.
Customer concentration is a second risk, particularly Lite-On’s large custom-ASIC exposure
and reliance on a relatively small number of hyperscalers. Program delays or share changes
can materially affect annual revenue.
Execution on capacity expansion also matters. Lite-On is investing substantially ahead of
forecast demand; if utilization ramps more slowly, depreciation and fixed operating costs

could limit expected margin improvement.
Competitive pricing remains a risk as more suppliers qualify for high-power products. We
believe the move toward larger, more engineered modules and systems should moderate
pure commodity pricing pressure, but it will not eliminate it.
Architecture is another source of uncertainty. A slower transition to external power racks is
not particularly damaging to our near-term thesis because 48/54V systems still require

substantial power content. Longer term, however, the precise mix between rack-level BBUs,
pooled UPS/BESS, ±400V, single-bus 800V and facility-level conversion will determine where
value accrues within the supply chain.
Rising competition and slower spec upgrades would impact earnings. Faster progress from
Chinese power suppliers, FX movements and weaker legacy IT/consumer demand could
create additional earnings variability.
HOLT methodology
The HOLT methodology does not assign ratings or a target price to a security. It is an analytical

tool that involves use of a set of proprietary quantitative algorithms and warranted value
calculations, collectively called the HOLT valuation model, that are consistently applied to all
the companies included in its database. The HOLT valuation model is a discounted cash flow
model. Third-party data (including consensus earnings estimates) are systematically translated
into a number of default variables and incorporated into the algorithms available in the HOLT

valuation model. The source financial statement, pricing, and earnings data provided by
outside data vendors are subject to quality control and may also be adjusted to more closely
measure the underlying economics of firm performance. These adjustments provide
consistency when analyzing a single company across time, or analyzing multiple companies
across industries or national borders. The default scenario that is produced by the HOLT
valuation model establishes a warranted price that represents the expected mean value for a

security based upon empirically derived fade algorithms that forecast a firm's future return on
capital and growth rates over an extended period of time. As the third-party data are
updated, the warranted price updates automatically. A company’s future achieved return on
capital or growth rate may differ from HOLT default forecast. Additional information about
the HOLT methodology is available upon request.
CFROI, CFROE, HOLT, HOLT Lens, HOLTfolio and “Powered by HOLT” are trademarks or

The HOLT Business Resilience Dashboard (HBRD) is constructed by applying systematic
processes on publicly available records and third-party data to generate quantitative
measures. The HBRD consistently assigns scores using a transparent combination of metrics
and weights across financial, environmental and governance inputs. Individual companies
can be scored on a global-size and/or region-size relative basis. The HBRD is also translated
into five textual assessments (e.g. “Highest” or “Lowest”) based on their quintile percentile

rank. For further details on the HBRD methodology HOLT Business Resilience Primer please
contact UBS Securities LLC, 11 Madison Avenue, New York, NY 10010, USA, Attention:
Investment Research. The HBRD does not constitute or imply any endorsement or
recommendation of any individual company. UBS has not sought to independently verify
information obtained from public and third-party sources and makes no representations or
warranties as to accuracy, completeness or reliability of such information due to inconsistent

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

研 究  報 告                
聯亞(3081.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
矽光需求持續強勁，更多設備投產與新增後段外包夥伴帶動產能倍增  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠CW Laser出貨量持續增加、新購顯影設備逐步進駐以及後段代工廠產能
開出，預估稅後EPS為21.01/49.3/97.15元，考量NPO和CPO技術逐步成熟且
聯亞具備多波⻑雷射領先技術，從2028年獲利評價，給予40倍本益比。 
 重點評論分析 
矽光產品出貨增加 ， 3Q26營收成⻑ ， 主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單 ， 4Q26營收攀升 ：
3Q26營收季增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模
擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，
主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之員工分紅費用，稅後EPS為
6.12元。4Q26矽光產品需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期
訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，預期矽光產品
出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。  
設備與後段代工產能開出， 2027和2028年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展
望2027~2028年，1.6T矽光產品已量產，3.2T與NPO/CPO應用之更高功率矽
光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中 ， 針對特定客戶之資料中心200G PD已
完成驗證且磊晶產能已規劃，預計未來數⽉將開始量產。目前E-Beam、奈米
壓印技術與DUV深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，隨更多E-Beam投
產，1Q27產能將明顯增加，奈米壓印設備預計於2026年底進廠。 
 投資風險 
設備與後段產能開出進度不如預期。

### Page 2

| 營運概況  
矽光產品出貨持續增加與後段產能開出，3Q26營收成⻑，主要客⼾已下⻑期訂單，4Q26營收持續攀升： 3Q26營收季
增27.9%至15.6億元 ， 係因矽光產品出貨量增加 ， 毛利率因營收規模擴大上升1.11ppts至58.6% ， 營業利益為7.71億元，
營業利益率為49.4%，營業費用較2Q26增加約1,400萬元至1.43億元，主要來自研發費用以及隨獲利增加按比例提列之
員工分紅費用，折舊費用較2Q26增加160萬元至7,660萬元，稅後淨利為6.21億元，稅後EPS為6.12元。4Q26矽光產品
需求持續強勁，主要客戶皆已開始下6個⽉以上的⻑期訂單，訂單能見度佳，隨公司本身與下游代工廠產能逐步開出，
預期矽光產品出貨量逐季成⻑，預估4Q26營收季增23.5%至19.3億元，稅後EPS為7.19元。影響毛利率因素有三：整體
營收與產出、匯率變化以及成本。管理層預期營收於接下來幾季將逐季成⻑，惟3Q26前半段台幣升值，隨後9⽉底台幣
轉貶值，匯率變化對3Q26毛利率產生些許不利影響，加上更多人員召募以及設備折舊成本逐步反映，且產品訂價方面，
聯亞僅維持賺取合理利潤，將無法持續大幅漲價，導致毛利率於接下來幾季成⻑幅度將趨緩。 
設備與後段代工產能開出，2027 和 2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%：展望 2027~2028 年，聯亞 1.6T 矽光產品
已經開始量產，3.2T 與 NPO/CPO 應用之更高功率矽光產品則和客戶持續合作開發和驗證中，另外過去受限產能，公
司設備主要用於生產 LD，自 2027 年開始，針對特定客戶之資料中心 200G 以上規格之 PD 已完成驗證，並已規劃相對
應磊晶產能，將於未來數⽉將開始量產，惟出貨量仍遠小於 LD。2026年 8 ⽉董事會決議與 1 美系資料中心客戶簽訂為
期 4 年之⻑期供貨合約，為滿足該客戶之需求，公司追加約 30 億元資本支出，該資本支出購置之設備可帶動 2027 和
2028 年總產量連續兩年皆年增 150%，另外因應磷化銦基板缺料問題，聯亞目前與 2 家日本主要供應商與 1 家美國主
要供應商達成未來數年的⻑期供貨合約，且美系客戶亦提供部分資金，協助確保後續大規模量產之基板供應無虞。因應
產能不足，聯亞持續新增後段外包合作夥伴位，台灣地區增加數家大型集團作為合作夥伴，該集團在光通訊領域投入意
願高，其資本支出承諾與實力遠高於早期合作夥伴，有助於與聯亞共同爭取國際 AI 訂單。曝光顯影製程方面，目前 E-
Beam、奈米壓印技術與 DUV 深紫外光微影三種技術皆同步進行擴產，首部新購置之 E-Beam 設備已於 2026 年 7 ⽉
開始量產，帶動 7 ⽉營收大幅躍升，隨更多 E-Beam 與 MOCVD 設備投產，1Q27 產能將有明顯增加且未來 8 個季度
產能將逐步增加 ， 奈米壓印設備預計於 2026 年底進場 ， DUV 深紫外光微影設備則於 2027 年以後加入生產 。 綜上所述 ，
預估 2026/2027/2028 年營收年增 155%/130%/86%至56.2/129.1/239.5 億元，稅後 EPS 為 21.01/49.3/97.15 元。

## Document 242: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

19/10/2026

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

## Document 324: COLA comfort for older consumers_ Older households’ spending has lagged, but the 2027 Social Security cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) could help narrow the gap. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/COLA comfort for older consumers_ Older households’ spending has lagged, but the 2027 Social Security cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) could help narrow the gap. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13034011     1

Economy 
COLA comfort for older consumers? 
27 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Older generations' spending growth has lagged other cohorts over the last year in Bank of America aggregated credit and debit 
card data, despite sizeable wealth gains. This likely partly reflects the highly uneven distribution of wealth among older 
households, limiting the extent to which rising wealth supports spending across the cohort. 
• More recently, stronger consumer spending appears to be primarily a labor-income story. However, older households are less 
exposed to accelerating wage growth. In addition, large differences in their income sources help explain the considerable 
variation in spending among this cohort. 
• The recently announced 3.6% Social Security cost-of-living adjustment could provide a meaningful boost in 2027. If inflation 
moderates as expected, benefit increases may outpace price growth, supporting older households' purchasing power and 
potentially narrowing their spending gap with other generations.

Older generations’ spending growth has lagged  
While total credit and debit card spending per household has picked up in 2026 in Bank of America internal data, a notable 
laggard has been the spending growth of older generations (Baby Boomers and Traditionalists). For around a year, discretionary 
spending growth for these older generations has been lower than that of overall households. This is in stark contrast to 2022-
2023, when they were significantly outstripping overall household spending growth (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Discretionary spending growth has been slower among older (Baby Boomer and Traditionalist) households despite wealth effects 
Total credit and debit card spending per household on discretionary categories by older generations and overall (three-month average, % YoY)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

I NSTI TUTE

### Page 2

2 27 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

On one level, this observation seems surprising given that in our piece on the older consumer we noted that a large and growing 
proportion of net worth is held by older cohorts (read: Entering the age of the older consumer). So what’s going on? One 
explanation is that wealth is very unevenly distributed even within the older cohort. Exhibit 2 uses the latest Federal Reserve 
(2025) Survey of Consumer Finances to illustrate that, across households older than 65 years of age, around two-thirds of net 
wealth is held by the wealthiest 10% of households. As a result, wealth gains from rising stock markets may not trickle too far 
down the wealth distribution of older consumers.  
Exhibit 2: The wealth distribution for older households is very skewed  
Distribution of net wealth for households over 65 years old (%)

Source: Federal Reserve Board of Governors (2025 Survey of Consumer Finances)  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Income and spending become more diverse and unequal with age 
Another important part of the story is that the rise in overall spending growth over the past year appears to be supported by 
labor market strength as well as rising wealth. The October Consumer Checkpoint described how a pickup in after-tax wage 
growth among lower- and middle-income households was likely a key factor driving a recovery in these cohorts’ spending growth 
(read: Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers). As households age, their income sources become more diverse (Exhibit 3), 
which means older generations will likely benefit less from this pickup in wage growth.  
Exhibit 3: As households age their sources of income become more diverse

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

spending. For example, using Bank of America internal data, Exhibit 4 shows that households over age 62 that rely solely on

The 2027 COLA may offer some respite  
Still in our view there are some reasons for optimism around older generations spending outcomes.  
For some older households in the middle of the income distribution, tax policy is likely providing some support to their spending 
growth. The One Big Beautiful Bill Act introduced a temporary deduction of up to $6,000 for each taxpayer aged 65 or older for 
2025-2028, with the benefit phasing out above $75,000 of modified adjusted gross income for single filers and $150,000 for 
joint filers. This measure is likely lifting after-tax income and spending capacity for older households – particularly those in the 
middle of the income distribution.  
However, households on lower incomes may see less benefit from these policies as they likely already owe limited federal 
income tax. For these households relying on Social Security either as their sole or as a large component of their income, the 
forthcoming annual cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) to social security benefits will likely be a more important determinant of 
their budgets for 2027. The announced COLA for 2027 is a rise of 3.6% (Exhibit 7) – the largest since 2023, though lower than 
the 8.7% rise in 2022. 
Exhibit 7: The 2027 COLA increase is the largest since 2023 and close to the historical average  
Social Security cost of living adjustments (% increase as of January effective date)

Source: US Social Security Administration (SSA)   
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The COLA increases are based on the year-over-year (YoY) % change between the third quarters of successive years in the 
Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers (CPI-W) and are typically announced in October and effective 
the following January. If the COLA increase simply keeps benefits in line with inflation, why is it particularly interesting? For sure, 
many times it is not. But when inflation is close to a turning point, the increase can end up looking relatively high or low 
compared with inflation over the course of the following year. The 2022 COLA increase was a case in point, as inflation in the 
subsequent year slowed sharply. In our view, this was one reason older generations’ spending growth was stronger than that of 
other age cohorts over 2022-2023.   
Exhibit 8: Inflation is expected to decline next year 
Consumer Price Index (CPI) (% YoY), third quarter CPI-W increases (red dots, % YoY) and latest consensus CPI forecast for 2027 (dotted line, % YoY)

Source: Haver Analytics, Bloomberg. Some fall 2025 months might be missing due to the government shutdown.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Regarding the 2027 COLA announcement, there are some parallels with 2022, albeit on a smaller scale. In particular, the 
consensus expectation is that CPI inflation is likely to cool over 2027, largely reflecting the lessening impact of higher gasoline 
prices (Exhibit 8).  
So if these expectations prove correct, households that rely on Social Security may receive a more impactful boost to their 
budgets next year than they have for the last few, potentially narrowing the gap between older generations’ spending and other 
households.  
What categories of spending growth might benefit from this? It is hard to be sure, but notable growth deficits for older 
generations currently reside in discretionary areas like electronics, lodging and restaurants (Exhibit 9). Some of these may reflect 
the preferences of older households, but it could be that there is room for some narrowing in these areas.  
Exhibit 9: Large spending growth gaps for older households exist in a range of discretionary goods and services

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Bank of America Proprietary Market Landscape Insights Study is an online quantitative survey among Bank of America customers and 
noncustomers sampled and balanced to provide a representative view of the U.S. adult population. Insights are based on aggregated and 
anonymized responses to surveys. Significance testing is done at the 90 percent confidence interval. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Style Exposure
We are incrementally positive on Jentech with multiple catalysts ahead. Near-term,

we believe the company’s 3Q26 earnings could be the first driver with a potential
GM beat, which should be reported in the second week of Nov. Longer-term, we
believe the drivers are from the incremental adoption of the removable lid in some
Nvidia Rubin GPUs in 2027 (in addition to Rubin Ultra), followed by the
meaningful Google TPU v9 spec upgrade potential in 2028 as another multi-fold
content increase story (based on JPMe), along with a structural margin expansion

trend given continued GPU and ASIC upgrades. Thus, we revise up our 2027E and
2028E EPS materially and are now 28% and 18% above the current BBGe
consensus.
• 3Q26 preview: expecting a stronger GM growth; 4Q26 outlook: slightly
better rev growth. We revise up our 3Q26 GM to 50.1% (vs current BBGe
consensus at 48.7%), which implies another quarter of strong GM expansion
(vs 2Q/1Q26 GM at 46.8%/41.8%), driven by better product mix to more AI
server business. Reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.5bn was largely in line with

the company’s guidance, and the 30% QoQ growth was mainly driven by the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the Rubin 1-piece lid shipment should remain
at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue momentum will
also continue, with incremental growth from small volume of removable lid
shipment. Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to grow 3%
QoQ, with GM further expanding thanks to ongoing mix enhancement.
• Further 2027 upside from incremental Rubin removable lid adoption;

2028 another earnings level-up year driven by meaningful upgrade in
TPU v9.We revise up our 2027E and 2028E revenue estimates materially by
22% and 14%, which are now 24% and 16% above consensus, respectively. For
the 2027 upward revision, it mainly reflects the incremental adoption of
removable lid (3-piece lid) in partial Rubin GPU from the next 1-2 quarter, in
addition to Rubin Ultra, as long as the company can ramp up its yield and
production of removable lid in the next few months with on-schedule capacity

expansion (new Taoyuan Aerotropolis plant) and equipment move-in (e.g. the
in-house screw locking equipment). More encouragingly, we believe 2028 will
see even greater earnings upside with Google TPU v9 likely upgrading its heat
spreader solution significantly to 2-piece or 3-piece lid design, in our view, as
the potential chip size (4-die) and TDP (potentially 2k+W or even higher)
should be a big level-up compared to v8, which makes the current stiffener-

only solution insufficient. As the content of 2-piece or 3-piece lid solution
could be 5-10x higher than the v8 stiffener, Google TPU v9 (with meaningful
volume) could drive the next wave of the earnings upgrade cycle for Jentech
from 2028 (following Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra as the major driver in 2027).
• Implications. We lift our 2027E and 2028E EPS by 28% and 17% to NT$193
and NT$295, respectively, reflecting the incremental removable lid adoption

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-
28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI

chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO switch
ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server
sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s technology
leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings
power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Jentech Precision Industrial Co. (Overweight; Price Target: NT
$8,800.00)
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component maker worldwide, with
leading production capabilities across heat spreader products (including stiffener and all
kinds of lids). We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-28, driven

by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new
solutions (e.g. CPO switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server sockets for chip customers. We
believe Jentech’s technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings power in the long term.
Valuation

Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which is largely in line with
Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the AI server boom started.
Risks to Rating and Price Target
Downside risks to our rating and price target include: 1) Potential delay in mainstream 3-
piece/removable lid design adoption, and 2) market share loss from competition.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

• The global landscape remains complex, shaped by AI, 
energy prices and tariffs. 
• Taiwan’s AI cycle remains strong, with robust AI 
exports and investment and an intact semiconductor 
competitive moat. 
• AI is generating broader domestic spillovers, with 
wealth effects and wage growth supporting 
consumption. 
• Taiwan is cushioning the energy shock by diversifying 
energy imports and absorbing much of the increase in 
upstream costs. 
• AI-driven growth is likely to moderate from 4Q26 into 
2027, as hyperscaler  cash-flow pressures rise and 
higher global rates lift investment costs. 
• Energy remains a n inflation risk, with domestic 
electricity prices facing upward pressure after the 
November local elections. 
• GDP growth is expected to moderate to high -single 
digits from 4Q26, while we maintain our 11.6%/5.6% 
GDP forecasts for 2026/27. 
• Inflation is likely to remain above 2% in 4Q26, with our 
2.0%/2.1% CPI forecasts for 2026/27 unchanged. 
• We maintain our call for CBC to hike 12.5bp to 2.125% 
in December, amid tighter liquidity conditions. 
• Housing should recover gradually despite credit 
easing, as excess new -home inventory remains a n 
overhang.

### Page 2

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

to 39% in 2027. Similarly, global 
semiconductor sales remained strong 
through 3Q, while WSTS expects growth 
to moderate from 90% this year to 27 % 
in 2027.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.

3) Tariffs

US tariff actions are likely to accelerate 
after the US midterm elections in 
November. Two major tariff measures 
remain in the pipeline. The Section 301 
forced-labor tariff was implemented in 
July, while final tariff action under the 
Section 301 overcapacity investigation, 
which covers 16 economies, remains 
pending. Meanwhile, Phase 1 of the 
Section 232 semiconductor tariffs was 
implemented in January, while Phase 2, 
covering a broader range of 
semiconductor-related products, 
remains pending.

Taiwan has secured a relatively 
favourable framework through its trade 
and investment agreements with the 
US. Taiwan signed an Agreement on 
Reciprocal Trade (ART) and an 
Investment MOU with the US earlier this 
year. The ART establishes a 15% US tariff 
rate on Taiwanese goods, while the MOU 
provides Taiwan with preferential 
treatment under Section 232, including 
for semiconductors. The ART has yet to 
enter into force, and it remains to be 
seen how the final Section 301 tariff 
burden will interact with the 15% ART 
framework. Meanwhile, major Taiwanese 
companies such as TSMC are expanding 
their US investments under the 
Investment MOU, which could support 
relatively favourable treatment for 
Taiwan when broader Section 232 
semiconductor tariffs are introduced.

Taiwan 4Q outlook

Taiwan has navigated the complex 
global environment relatively well so far 
this year, supported by robust AI exports 
and investment, diversified energy 
imports, and efforts to absorb higher 
upstream energy costs. Some 
normalisation is likely in 4Q26 –2027, as 
the AI -driven growth boost moderates 
and domestic energy prices face greater 
pressure to adjust.

1) Growth

GDP growth remains broadly on track 
with our expectations. High -frequency 
indicators suggest that GDP growth 
should remain around 12% yoy in 3Q, 
broadly in line with 12.9% in 2Q but below 
the 15.4% peak in 1Q. Growth is expected 
to moderate to a high -single-digit pace 
from 4Q onwards. We maintain our full -
year GDP growth forecasts at 11.6% for 
2026 and 5.6% for 2027.

The AI cycle remains the key driver of 
Taiwan's growth.  Information and 
communication products and electronic 
components accounted for nearly 80% 
of total exports in the first nine months 
of this year, while manufacturing 
accounts for nearly 50% of gross fixed

capital formation. From 4Q onwards, 
export growth is expected to moderate 
from exceptionally strong levels to a 
more normal but still robust pace, 
broadly following the trajectory of 
global hyperscaler capex and 
semiconductor sales. Investment is likely 
to follow a similar path.

For now, Taiwanese technology 
companies continue to report robust 
revenue growth, led by semiconductors, 
ODMs/EMS, and PCs and servers. 
Capacity utilisation at leading Taiwanese 
foundries has also continued to rise, 
suggesting that investment demand 
remains strong.

The AI boom is also beginning to 
generate broader spillovers to the 
domestic economy. The AI-driven equity 
rally has generated significant wealth 
effects, with the TAIEX up 70% year-to-
date and Taiwan becoming the world's 
fifth-largest stock market. Taiwanese 
households have benefited from the 
appreciation of financial assets, with 
equities and mutual funds accounting for 
nearly 30% of total household financial 
assets. The equity rally has also 
generated positive income effects. Wage 
growth has picked up, led by the services 
sector, particularly financial services, as 
strong earnings at financial 
institutions—especially securities 
firms—have prompted higher 
compensation to retain talent. These 
wealth and income gains should support 
household consumption, creating a 
positive feedback loop that broadens the 
benefits of the AI boom across the wider 
economy.

2) Inflation

CPI inflation is also tracking broadly in 
line with our forecast. Headline CPI 
remained elevated at 2.4% yoy in 3Q, up 
from 1.2% in 1Q and 2.2% in 2Q. We 
maintain our forecast for headline CPI 
inflation to remain close to 2.5% in 4Q, 
and retain our full-year forecasts at 2.0% 
for 2026 and 2.1% for 2027.

Energy inflation remains the main 
upside risk.  The government has 
absorbed much of the increase in energy 
costs through state -owned CPC and 
Taipower, limiting the pass -through to 
domestic consumers. With losses at both 
companies widening, the government 
will need to decide whether to provide 
additional subsidies to cover the losses 
or allow further increases in domestic 
energy prices. The September re view 
postponed the adjustment to the 
December meeting, when the overall 
situation will be reassessed after the 
November local elections. Taipower's  
latest review indicated that electricity 
rates would need to rise by 12.8%. A hike 
in electricity prices could lift inflation 
expectations and generate second -
round effects, particularly as the labour 
market tightens and wage pressures 
increase.

3) Monetary policy

The central bank kept the policy 
discount rate unchanged at 2.00% at its 
September meeting, in line with our 
expectations. We continue to expect a 
modest 12.5bp rate hike at the 
December meeting, bringing the policy 
rate to 2.125%.  By then, CPI inflation is 
likely to have remained above 2% for 
around six consecutive months. The 
possibility of an increase in electricity 
prices following the November local 
elections could further lift inflation 
expectations, strengthening the case for 
a December rate hike.

Financial conditions have already 
tightened despite the absence of a 
policy rate hike.  M2 growth remained 
around 7% yoy in 3Q, well below double-
digit nominal GDP growth. At the same 
time, bank loan growth has accelerated 
to more than 10% yoy, driven by strong

corporate credit demand, particularly AI-

are also evident in rising market interest

and government bond yields.

The central bank has continued to ease 
housing credit controls gradually. At its 
September meeting, it raised the LTV 
cap on second -home mortgage loans to 
70%, the second easing of housing credit 
controls this year. There is scope for 
further easing, including removing the 
LTV cap on second-home loans or raising 
the LTV caps for third - and fourth-home 
loans and high -value properties, which 
currently stand at 30%. However, the 
central bank is likely to proceed 
cautiously, given concerns that a faster 
relaxation could reignite  speculative 
demand and put renewed upward 
pressure on housing prices.

4) Housing

A gradual and modest recovery in the 
housing market is expected from 4Q26 
into 2027.  The existing -home market 
has already shown early signs of 
recovery, with Sinyi  housing prices 
returning to slightly positive yoy growth 
since 2Q. Total building transactions also 
returned to positive yoy growth in 3Q. 
The easing of credit controls should help 
reinforce this modest recovery trend.

The new -home market remains under 
pressure. The number of new units under 
construction has started to decline since 
2023, while new completions are  only 
beginning to ease this year . Demand 
remains weak, as potential buyers 
continue to wait for lower prices. Unsold 
new homes stood at 113K units at end -
2025, compared with a historical normal 
level of around 80K, implying excess 
inventory of roughly 30K units. At the 
current pace of new -home transactions, 
it would take roughly one year to 
normalize the existing excess inventory, 
and longer when factoring in the new 
supply pipeline. Some industry experts 
estimate that it could take as long as 3.8 
years digest current inventories fully 
(here).

## Document 430: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

## Document 554: マレーシア中銀は８会合連続で金利据え置き、先行きは利上げ転換も視野に ～AI・半導体需要が景気を支えるなか、2027年の物価上昇_3ea2f490

Asia Trends

マレーシア中銀は８会合連続で金利据え置き、先行きは利上げ転換も視野に 
～AI・半導体需要が景気を支えるなか、2027 年の物価上昇圧力の強まりが政策判断のカギに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ マレーシア中銀は、11 月５日に開催した定例会合において、 政策金利を８会合連続で2.75％
に据え置くことを決定した。同国経済は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大を追い風に
堅調に推移し、７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率（速報値）は前年同期比＋5.3％となった。中
銀は2026 年の成長率が５％を上回るなど、景気の先行きに強気の見方を示している。 
○ 一方、９月のインフレ率は同＋1.8％と落ち着いた推移が続いているものの、2027 年に向け
て物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性を指摘している。また、通貨リンギは輸出拡大による貿易黒
字の拡大などを背景に底堅く推移している。とはいえ、先行きも米国の金融政策や国際金融
市場の動向といった外部環境に左右されやすい転嫁が続くと見込まれる。 
○ 中銀は当面、金利据え置きによる様子見姿勢を維持すると予想される。さらに、景気の堅調
さが賃金や物価の持続的な上昇につながる場合には、2027 年前半にも利上げに転じる可能性
もある。ただし、世界経済の減速や地政学リスクなどの不確実性も残るため、今後の金融政
策は景気・物価動向や為替相場を慎重に見極めながら運営されると考えられる。

【マレーシア中銀は８会合連続で金利据え置き、景気と物価を慎重に見極める姿勢を維持】 
マレーシア中央銀行 （ＢＮＭ） は、11 月
５日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会
（ＭＰＣ）において、政策金利である翌
日物政策金利 （ＯＰＲ）を2.75％に据え
置くことを決定した （図１） 。 同行は、2025
年７月の定例会合において、米国の関税
政策などを巡る不確実性の高まりを背景
に、25bp の利下げに踏み切った。 その後
は金利を据え置く様子見姿勢に転じてお
り、今回の金利据え置きは８会合連続と
なる。同国経済を巡っては、世界的なＡ

### Page 2

2/4 
Ｉ（人工知能） ・半導体関連需要の拡大を追い風に輸出の堅調な拡大が続き、 国内の雇用・所得環境
も底堅く推移している。一方、インフレ率は落ち着いた推移が続いており、中銀は景気の底堅さと
物価の安定を踏まえ、現行の金融政策を維持することが適切と判断したと考えられる。 
会合後に公表された声明文では、 世界経済について「技術関連投資の拡大や安定した雇用環境を
背景に底堅さが続いている」との認識を示した。 一方で、「エネルギー価格の上昇に伴うコスト増加
が消費者物価に波及し、 世界的なインフレ圧力は根強く残る」と指摘した。 また、「地政学リスクの
長期化に加え、 各国の金融政策調整に伴う金融環境の引き締まり、 金融市場における資産価格の割
高感、 財政面の脆弱性などが世界経済の下振れリスクになる」 とした。ただし、「技術関連投資の一
段の拡大やサプライチェーンの回復が、景気を押し上げる可能性 」にも言及した。こうしたなか、
中銀は現行の金利水準について「物価の安定と持続可能な経済成長の見通しに整合的」との評価を
維持しており、今後も景気と物価を巡るリスクのバランスを慎重に見極める姿勢を示した。

【ＡＩ・半導体需要の拡大を追い風に景気は堅調、2026 年の成長率は５％を上回る見通し】 
足元のマレーシア経済は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大を追い風に、堅調な景気拡大が
続いている。 ７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長
率 （速報値） は前年同期比＋5.3％と前期
（同＋6.0％） から鈍化したものの、 高い
成長率を維持していることが確認された
（図２） 。前期比年率ベースでは＋6.21％
と試算されるなど、足元の景気は堅調に
推移している様子がうかがえる。産業別
では、サービス業で底堅い動きが続いた
ほか、製造業では伸びが加速しており、
なかでも電気・電子部品関連を中心とす
る輸出志向型産業の拡大が製造業の成長
をけん引する動きがみられた。また、建設業も堅調に推移しており、設備投資やデータセンター関
連投資の拡大も景気を下支えしている。 
外需についても、９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋54.5％と大幅に拡大した。世界的なＡＩ・半導体
関連投資の拡大を受けた同関連の旺盛な需要に加え、石油製品や機械・設備、ＬＮＧなどの輸出も
押し上げに寄与している。こうした外需の堅調さに加えて、国内においても、安定した雇用環境や
継続的な投資活動が内需を支えている。中銀は今回の声明文で、「2026 年の経済成長率が５％を上
回る」との見通しを示し、 前回９月会合時点の見通し （５％程度）から上方修正した。 さらに、2027
年についても、「技術関連需要の拡大や観光収入の増加に加え、2027 年度予算に盛り込まれた家計
支援策などが個人消費を下支えする」との見方を示した。一方で、イラン情勢の悪化が長期化した
場合の影響や資源生産の減少などには、引き続き警戒が必要である。

### Page 3

3/4 
【インフレ率は落ち着いた推移が続くも、2027 年には物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性を指摘】 
前述のように堅調な景気が続いているにもかかわらず、 インフレ率は落ち着いた推移をみせてい
る。 ９月のインフレ率は前年同月比＋1.8％となり、 前月 （同＋1.9％） から伸びが鈍化した （図３） 。
住居・水道・電気・ガス・その他燃料関連の伸びが鈍化したほか、食品・飲料関連の物価上昇圧力
も限定的となっている。さらに、変動の大きい品目などを除いたコアインフレ率は同＋1.7％と前
月から横ばいで推移しており、幅広い品目に物価上昇圧力が広がる動きとはなっていない。 
ただし、 先行きの物価動向を巡っては
不透明感が残る。イラン情勢の悪化によ
る原油をはじめと する国際商品市況の
高止まりは、 輸送費や製造コストの上昇
を通じて国内物価を押し上げる要因と
なる。 また、 異常気象による食料品供給
への影響に加え、 旺盛な内需が企業によ
るコスト転嫁を容易にする可能性にも
注意が必要である。今回の声明文では、
「2027 年に向けて総合・コアの双方で
インフレ率が上昇する 」と予想する一
方、「政府による政策対応や、安定した需要環境などを理由に上昇は抑制されたものにとどまる」と
の見方を示した。 このため、 足元では早急な金融引き締めを必要とする状況には至っていないもの
の、物価上昇圧力の持続性が今後の政策判断を左右することになると考えられる。

【リンギ相場は底堅い動きをみせるも、引き続き米国の金融政策や金融市場の動向に左右される】 
このところの金融市場においては、 通
貨リンギ の対米ドル 相場は比較的底堅
く推移している（図４）。背景には、 世界
的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大を受け
た輸出の好調を受けて、貿易黒字が拡大
するなど、実需面からリンギが買われや
すい環境が続いていることがある。一
方、 国際金融市場における投資家のリス
ク選好の変化や米ドル相場の動向も、 リ
ンギ相場を左右する要因となっている
ことにも注意が必要である。 
このため、先行きのリンギ相場には上下双方のリスクが存在する。 マレーシア経済の堅調な拡大
や輸出・対外収支の改善はリンギ相場の下支え要因となる一方、 ＦＲＢ（米連邦準備制度理事会）
による金融引き締めが長期化すれば、米国との金利差がリンギ相場の上値を抑える可能性がある。

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
また、イラン情勢が再び悪化するなど国際金融市場でリスク回避姿勢が強まれば、多くの新興国通
貨と同様にリンギにも売り圧力がかかることが懸念される。 さらに、 世界的な半導体需要が一服す
る場合には、 輸出の伸びの鈍化を通じてリンギ相場の下押し要因となる可能性もある。 こうしたな
か、中銀が今後、金融引き締め方向への政策転換を示唆するかどうかは、市場の金利見通しを通じ
てリンギ相場に影響すると予想される。

【2027 年は金融引き締めへの転換も視野に入るも、追加引き締めには物価上昇の持続性がカギ】 
先行きの金融政策を巡っては、 当面は現行の金利水準を維持しつつ、2027 年には金融引き締めが
選択肢に浮上する可能性が考えられる。足元の同国経済は、 外需の堅調さや内需の底堅さを背景に
５％程度の成長を維持しており、金融緩和を一段と強化する必要性は小さいと見込まれる。一方、
足元のインフレ率は比較的落ち着いているものの、中銀は 2027 年にかけて物価上昇圧力が強まる
との見方を示している。 こうした状況を勘案すれば、 将来的に金融引き締めへの転換を検討する余
地はある。ただし、今回の声明文は利上げの実施時期を示唆したものではなく、金融政策の方向性
が明確に転換したと判断するのは早計であろう。 
今後の政策運営は、 外需主導による景気拡大の動きが、国内の賃金や物価上昇圧力へと波及する
かどうかがカギを握る。世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大が続くとともに、内需も堅調に推移
し、 インフレ率が持続的に上昇する場合には、 中銀が2027 年前半にも25bp 程度の利上げに踏み切
る可能性がある。一方、イラン情勢の緊迫化による世界経済の減速や、米国の金融引き締めの影響
などを受けて輸出や設備投資が鈍化する場合には、 金利据え置きによる様子見姿勢が一段と長期化
することも考えられる。このため、先行きの金融政策は、景気動向のみならず、輸入物価や企業に
よるコスト転嫁の広がり、 さらにはリンギ相場を含む金融市場の動向を総合的に勘案しつつ、 慎重
に運営されると予想される。

## Document 593: 京东收购Ceconomy获批

Source PDF: all report pdf/京东收购Ceconomy获批.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

11 ⽉ 3 ⽇，欧盟委员会依据《外国补贴条例》附条件批准京东收购德国消费电⼦零售商
Ceconomy 。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 3 ⽇，欧盟委员会依据《外国补贴条例》（ Foreign Subsidies Regulation ，下称 FSR ）附条
件批准京东收购德国消费电⼦零售商 Ceconomy 。京东须在未来 10 年内，按与 Ceconomy 相同的
条件向第三⽅零售商提供仓储物流和技术服务，集团内部交易须遵循市场条件。承诺履⾏情况
由独⽴受托⼈监督。
欧委会在调查中认定，京东获得了来⾃中国的优惠融资、税收优惠和补助等形式的外国补贴，
可能扭曲欧盟内部市场。这些补贴与交易完成后将提供给 Ceconomy 的物流和技术能⼒相关，还
可能使京东及合并后实体更容易为其在欧盟市场的业务融资，并通过激进定价对其他市场参与
者造成不利影响。
为消除上述关切，京东作出三项承诺。
⼀是京东与 Ceconomy 之间的集团内部交易按市场条件进⾏，包括京东向 Ceconomy 提供的融
资。
⼆是京东向第三⽅零售商提供仓储物流服务的条件，与提供给 Ceconomy 的条件相同。

三是技术服务以同等条件向第三⽅提供。
欧委会称，经市场测试，双⽅针对竞争对⼿提出的主要短板，强化了集团内部交易与融资相关
承诺，并改善了第三⽅获得物流与技术服务的条款。欧委会认定，这些承诺⾜以消除补贴造成
的扭曲，能够阻⽌京东向 Ceconomy 交叉补贴，并消除合并后实体凭借受补贴的物流与技术能⼒
获得的竞争优势。承诺有效期为 10 年，由独⽴受托⼈监督，欧委会负责监管。
这笔交易于 2025 年 7 ⽉宣布。京东以每股 4.6 欧元现⾦向 Ceconomy 全体股东发出收购要约，按
报价计算， Ceconomy 股权价值约 22 亿欧元。
2025 年 11 ⽉ 27 ⽇追加接受期结束后，京东于 12 ⽉ 2 ⽇公布最终结果，获得约 59.8% 的股本和表决
权。加上创始家族投资公司 Convergenta 保留的 25.35% 股份，双⽅合计持股约 85.2% 。
截⾄ 2025 年 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇， Ceconomy 旗下 MediaMarkt 、 MediaWorld 和 Saturn 等品牌在 11 个欧洲国

家运营 1067 家⻔店。
按 FSR 规则，交易参与⽅中⾄少有⼀家在欧盟设⽴且在欧盟营业额达到 5 亿欧元，同时交易各⽅
在交易协议签署、公开要约宣布或取得控制权三者中最早⼀项之前三年内，从第三国获得合计
达到 5000 万欧元的财务贡献的，须向欧委会申报。欧委会于 2026 年 5 ⽉ 28 ⽇对本案⽴案深⼊调
查，随后向京东发出理由声明。
8 ⽉ 19 ⽇，中国司法部发布第 8 号公告，认定欧盟在京东调查中对中国实体采取的相关跨境调查
做法构成不当域外管辖，任何组织和个⼈不得执⾏或协助执⾏。京东此前表⽰，本次收购资⾦
来⾃外部私⼈银⾏债务和⽇常经营活动产⽣的可⽤现⾦。

后欧盟持续监督的重点。

## Document 602: 債券市場總評：投等債吸引力增

台北富邦銀行
